What brings you in today?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 55 / carpenter / associates\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Loss_of_Interest\nmanifestation: \"What brings me in is that I have stopped wanting to do the things that usually keep me going, like salsa nights, church gatherings, cooking for the neighbors, or even walking into my shop just to enjoy the smell of the wood.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Reserved patient.\nA reserved patient may\n1) provide brief, vague, or evasive answers to questions about their symptoms and experiences,\n2) be reluctant to answer questions about personal information, emotions, or sensitive clinical experiences, and\n3) refrain from voluntarily providing information beyond what the doctor specifically asks.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

It’s just not there right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this loss of interest is mainly because of your wife’s health worsening?\n\nPatient: Maybe partly… I don’t know. What I know is I’ve stopped wanting to do the things that usually keep me going—salsa, church gatherings, cooking for neighbors… even just walking into my shop to smell the wood. It’s all kind of gone quiet.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days. Especially in the morning. I wake up with this heavy, empty feeling in my chest, and I have to kind of hold it together so I don’t break down in front of my wife.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings been making it hard for you to manage your usual daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I’ve had to let other guys take over parts of jobs I’d normally watch myself. And I’ve kind of stopped showing up for people—church, friends, neighbors. It got bad enough that my wife and someone from the parish got worried about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’ve had thoughts like that. Not so much… I don’t know… not wanting to say it out loud. Sometimes I think maybe it’d be easier if I just didn’t wake up. And then I catch myself thinking my family might be better off without me.\n\nThat scares me.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about how you might kill yourself?\n\nPatient: Not… not in a specific way, no. It’s more like I think maybe it’d be easier if I didn’t wake up, or that my family might be better off without me. And then I get scared that I’m even thinking that.\n\nDoctor: Before this started two months ago, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt depressed like this for days or weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I guess… I’m not sure about before, but this time it’s been going on longer than two months. More like… six months. It doesn’t feel like just a rough week or regular stress anymore.\n\nDoctor: When you have a moment away from your wife’s health situation, does the depressed feeling still stay with you?\n\nPatient: Yeah. It’s still there.\n\nEven when nothing is happening right that second… most mornings I wake up with this heavy, empty feeling. I’ve had to let other guys take over parts of jobs I’d normally watch myself. And I’ve kind of stopped showing up for people—church, friends, neighbors. It got bad enough that my wife and someone from the parish got worried about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’ve had thoughts like that. Not so much… I don’t know… not wanting to say it out loud. Sometimes I think maybe it’d be easier if I just didn’t wake up. And then I catch myself thinking my family might be better off without me.\n\nThat scares me.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about how you might kill yourself?\n\nPatient: Not… not in a specific way, no. It’s more like I think maybe it’d be easier if I didn’t wake up, or that my family might be better off without me. And then I get scared that I’m even thinking that.\n\nDoctor: Before this started two months ago, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt depressed like this for days or weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I guess… I’m not sure about before, but this time it’s been going on longer than two months. More like… six months. It doesn’t feel like just a rough week or regular stress anymore.\n\nDoctor: When you have a moment away from your wife’s health situation, does the depressed feeling still stay with you?\n\nPatient: Yeah. It’s still there.\n\nEven when nothing is happening right that second… most mornings I wake up with this heavy, empty feeling. Like the whole house has gone gray, and I’m just trying to keep it together so my wife doesn’t see me fall apart.\n\nDoctor: Before this current stretch, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt depressed most days for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know. Nothing like this that I can clearly point to. Maybe I’ve had low times, but… not something I can say was most days for two weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have.\n\nI keep going over everything with my wife—appointments, little changes I missed—and I tell myself maybe if I’d been a better husband, I would’ve caught it sooner or somehow protected her.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble with sleep lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that.\n\nI mean… sleep hasn’t felt normal, I guess, but I haven’t been keeping track of it. Some nights are just… harder than others.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a noticeable change in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I haven’t really been paying attention to it. Maybe a little different, but nothing I can say for sure.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I wake up with this heavy, empty feeling in my chest, and I have to kind of hold it together so I don’t break down in front of my wife.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings been making it hard for you to manage your usual daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I’ve had to let other guys take over parts of jobs I’d normally watch myself. And I’ve kind of stopped showing up for people—church, friends, neighbors. It got bad enough that my wife and someone from the parish got worried about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’ve had thoughts like that. Not so much… I don’t know… not wanting to say it out loud. Sometimes I think maybe it’d be easier if I just didn’t wake up. And then I catch myself thinking my family might be better off without me.\n\nThat scares me.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about how you might kill yourself?\n\nPatient: Not… not in a specific way, no. It’s more like I think maybe it’d be easier if I didn’t wake up, or that my family might be better off without me. And then I get scared that I’m even thinking that.\n\nDoctor: Before this started two months ago, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt depressed like this for days or weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I guess… I’m not sure about before, but this time it’s been going on longer than two months. Like the whole house has gone gray, and I’m just trying to keep it together so my wife doesn’t see me fall apart.\n\nDoctor: Before this current stretch, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt depressed most days for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know. Nothing like this that I can clearly point to. Some nights are just… harder than others.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a noticeable change in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I haven’t really been paying attention to it. Maybe a little different, but nothing I can say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Before this started getting worse, had you already been feeling down or low most days for at least two years?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure.
